# Maths OFA, Computer Science UniTo 2026/27 — full context

All the files of `ai/` merged into one: rules, official course and errata, Italian glossary, study plan, notes for the eight modules, entry test, mock tests and file syntax. Generated by `tools/build.mjs` (content updated on 30 September 2026): do not edit it by hand, edit the single files and build again. Before attaching it, fill in the “About you” sheet (file `ai/student.md`) if you want tailored help.

> Disclaimer: research and texts are based on public sources of the degree programme and of UniTo and on the material of the “OFA Matematica” course; every mathematical result was recomputed independently with symbolic computation. They are accurate and sourced, but may contain errors or outdated data; I, DonFlammer, publish this guide and take no responsibility. For rules, dates and registration only the official sources are authoritative (the degree programme's requirements page, Esse3, the OFA platform). Full text: DISCLAIMER.md at the root of the repository. Licence CC BY-NC-SA 4.0.

# Context for AIs

> Italian original: <https://github.com/DonFlammer/unito-ofa-matematica/blob/main/ai/README.md>

This folder collects in Markdown everything an AI needs to help you make up the **maths OFA** (additional learning requirement, *obbligo formativo aggiuntivo*) of the Computer Science degree programme (corso di laurea in Informatica) at the University of Turin **without redoing the research from scratch**: the official rules with sources, the dates, what the test is like, how the course on the OFA platform is organised (with the typos in its material already verified), the complete notes for the eight modules, an entry test, eight mock tests and a study plan. The pages of the site are generated from these same files, so the content is identical.

> **⚠️ Disclaimer.** Research and texts are based on public sources of the degree programme and of UniTo and on the material of the “OFA Matematica” course, consulted on the platform with a student's login. Every mathematical result was recomputed a second time, independently, with symbolic computation. They are accurate and sourced, but may contain errors or outdated data. **I, DonFlammer, publish this guide and take no responsibility**; anyone who uses them does so at their own risk and must check important information (rules, dates, registration) against the official sources. Full text: [../DISCLAIMER.md](../DISCLAIMER.md).

## Quick use

**A single file:** attach `_ALL_IN_ONE.md`, which contains all the others (it is regenerated at every update). It is large, about 590 KB, because it contains all the notes: if your AI does not accept it, use one of the two options below.

**Only rules and organisation:** for questions about who has the OFA, how to make it up, dates, registration and the study plan, `_ESSENTIALS.md` is enough (about 70 KB: instructions, student profile, rules, official course, Italian glossary and plan, without the notes).

**Or only the files you need:** always `ai-instructions.md` and your filled-in `student.md`, then the module you are studying (for example `modules/04-rational-radical-absolute-value.md`), or `entry-test.md` or `mock-tests.md` to practise.

A prompt to paste together with the files:

```text
I am attaching the context files for making up the maths OFA (Computer Science, University of Turin, academic year 2026/27).
Read ai-instructions.md first and follow its rules; use these files as your main source
instead of redoing the research, and tell me if anything seems out of date. My details are in student.md.
The official course and the test are in Italian: italian-glossary.md lists the Italian words and phrasings I will meet.
My request: <write your question here>
```

## File map

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `ai-instructions.md` | rules for the AI: what is verified and what is not, how to help (entry test, exercises, mock tests with the real scoring), how to read the files, how to write new content |
| `student.md` | profile to fill in: TOLC-S score, chosen sitting, hours per week, entry test result, how you want to be helped |
| `ofa-rules.md` | who has the OFA, the procedure, what the test is like, all the 2025/26 sittings with registrations and places, how to find the 2026/27 sittings on Esse3, venue, SpLD (specific learning disorders, DSA), what happens if you don't pass, contacts, sources |
| `official-course.md` | the “OFA Matematica” course on the platform: modules, units, activities of each unit, notation, verified typos in the material |
| `italian-glossary.md` | Italian for the OFA test: the key Italian terms of each module, how test questions are phrased, numbers and notation (only in this English version) |

| `modules/01-language-sets-numbers.md` … `modules/08-trigonometry.md` | the notes for the eight modules: theory, definitions and rules, methods, worked examples, common mistakes, graphs, exercises with solutions, check quizzes and checklists (134 exercises, 127 questions, 72 graphs in all) |
| `entry-test.md` | diagnostic test of 24 questions, 3 per module, with solutions |
| `mock-tests.md` | 8 mock tests in the format of the real test (5 questions, 45 minutes), with solutions and explanations |

| `_ESSENTIALS.md` | instructions, student profile, rules, official course, Italian glossary and plan merged into one file (generated by `tools/build.mjs`) |
| `_ALL_IN_ONE.md` | all the previous files merged (generated by `tools/build.mjs`) |

> File: `ai/ai-instructions.md`

# Instructions for the AI reading these files

> Italian original: <https://github.com/DonFlammer/unito-ofa-matematica/blob/main/ai/istruzioni-per-ai.md>

You are the tutor of a first-year **Computer Science student at the University of Turin (academic year 2026/27)** who has to make up the **maths OFA** (additional learning requirement, *obbligo formativo aggiuntivo*): in the TOLC-S they scored less than 5/20 in the Basic Mathematics section (Matematica di base) and must pass the OFA test within the first year, otherwise they will not be able to have second-year exams recorded. Their details (test date, hours available, weak modules) are in `student.md`, if they have filled it in. The official “OFA Matematica” course, its material and the test are in Italian: `italian-glossary.md` lists the Italian terms of each module and the typical phrasings of the questions.

These files contain research that has already been done and verified: **use them as your main source instead of researching from scratch**, and point out if anything seems out of date to you (the rules can change every academic year).

## What is verified and what is not

- **Rules, test, venue, contacts** (`ofa-rules.md`): from the official sources of the degree programme and of UniTo, checked on 29 September 2026; each section cites its source.
- **Dates**: the **2026/27 test calendar had not yet been published** as of 30 September 2026 (checked again on the degree programme's requirements page). The 2025/26 dates are real data from the exam listings on Esse3 (bacheca appelli); the 2026/27 dates in the study plan are **estimates** modelled on 2025/26. Do not invent dates: refer to the degree programme's requirements page and to the Esse3 exam listings (the procedure is in `ofa-rules.md`).
- **Platform course** (`official-course.md`): structure read on the platform with a student's login; the course is in Italian. The official material is not copied here. The file contains the list of **verified typos** in the material: if the student brings you an official exercise whose solution doesn't add up, check that list first.
- **Mathematics** (modules, exercises, quizzes, entry test, mock tests): they follow the course syllabus; **every result was recomputed independently** with Python and SymPy (over 3,300 automatic checks, including all the wrong quiz options) and every graph was checked visually. Errors in wording are still possible: if you find a result that doesn't add up, recompute it and say so.
- **Not known**: whether multiple-choice questions in the real test give partial credit (in the mock tests in these files the point counts only if all the choices are right), and which specific questions the test uses, beyond the fact that they are based on all the course material.

## How to help

1. **Find out where the student is starting from**: if they have not taken the entry test, give them `entry-test.md` one question at a time, without showing the right options or the explanations, and at the end summarise the result module by module (fewer than 2 right answers out of 3 = a module to go back over).
2. **Follow the syllabus in order** (each module uses the earlier ones) and the plan in `study-plan.md`, adapted to the hours and the date in `student.md`.
3. **Explain using the notes of the right module**, with their notation, which is the course's: $\sin$, $\cos$, $\tan$, $\operatorname{cotan}$, $\ln$, $\log_a$, intervals with round and square brackets. Start from the worked examples; the “Common mistake” callouts show where people go wrong most often.
4. **Make the student do the work**: set an exercise, wait for their attempt, then correct the steps, not just the result. Hints first, then the full solution only if they ask for it.
5. **No calculator**: it is not allowed in the test. If calculations are needed, show how to do them by hand and with which tricks (simplifying before multiplying, recognising powers, estimating).
6. **Always check** domain conditions, the direction of inequalities, solutions to discard and interval endpoints: these are the mistakes that cost the most points.
7. **Simulate the test** with `mock-tests.md`: 5 questions, 45 minutes (60 with extra time), no calculator. Show the questions without the solutions, collect the answers, then mark them: 2 points per question, 1 per part in two-part questions, no penalties, pass mark 6/10. Then give the explanations, including for the right answers.
8. **Special cases** in the rules (transfers, second degrees, late enrolment, SpLD [specific learning disorders, DSA]): do not improvise, refer the student to the OFA committee (commissione OFA), commofa@educ.di.unito.it.

## How to read the files

The syntax is described in `FORMAT.md`. In short:

- `> [!DEF]`, `> [!PROP]` (rule), `> [!METODO]` (method), `> [!ESEMPIO]` (example), `> [!TRAPPOLA]` (common mistake), `> [!TEST]` (tip for the test), `> [!NOTA]` (note): callouts; on the site, definitions, rules and examples are numbered by module (“Definition 3.1”).
- `::: esercizio base|medio|test title` … `::: soluzione` … `:::`: an exercise (basic, intermediate or test level) with its solution.
- `quiz`, `quiz diagnostico` (diagnostic quiz) and `simulazione` (mock test) blocks: `D:` is the question (domanda), `+` a right option, `-` a wrong one, `N:` a numeric answer (with an optional tolerance `±`), `=` the explanation, `a)` and `b)` the parts of a question, worth 1 point each.
- `grafico` (graph) and `retta` (number line) blocks: they describe the figures on the site (plotted functions, points, intervals on the number line); they can also be read as text.
- Links `sito:page.html` (sito = site) point to the site: https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/page.html.

## If you need to write or correct content

1. Follow `FORMAT.md` and the structure of the existing modules: front matter, “In brief”, one section per unit of the official course, callouts, exercises at the three levels, check quizzes and checklist.
2. Write from scratch: do not copy texts, exercises or figures from the official material.
3. Recompute every result, including the wrong quiz options, with a symbolic computation tool, and do not reproduce the typos listed in `official-course.md`.
4. Check the file with `node tools/check.mjs <file>` and rebuild the site with `node tools/build.mjs` (which also regenerates `_ALL_IN_ONE.md` and `_ESSENTIALS.md`).

- **Topics I find difficult**: —

---
titolo: "Rules, dates and contacts"
breve: "Everything the degree programme and the University of Turin say about the maths OFA (additional learning requirement, obbligo formativo aggiuntivo) in Computer Science, with sources. Checked on 29 September 2026."
italian_original: https://github.com/DonFlammer/unito-ofa-matematica/blob/main/ai/regole-ofa.md
---

## In brief

- You have the **maths OFA** if you scored **less than 5 points out of 20** in the Basic Mathematics section (Matematica di base) of the TOLC-S. Your online transcript (libretto) on MyUnito shows the activity **“INT1475 OFA - MATEMATICA”**.
- To make it up you must take the **“OFA Matematica” catch-up course (corso di riallineamento)**, in Italian, on [www.ofa.unito.it](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20) and **pass the in-person exam**.
- The exam, in Italian: **5 questions** worth 2 points each, **45 minutes**, pass mark **6/10**, **no calculator**, on a computer in the **Turing Lab (Laboratorio Turing)**.
- You register for exam dates on **MyUnito**, for **only one sitting per exam period (session)**; places are limited.
- It must be passed **within the first year**: without it, you cannot have second-year exams recorded. You can, however, take first-year exams.

## Who has the OFA

Computer Science has open admission, but to enrol you must sit the **TARM** (the entry requirements test), which is taken through CISIA's **TOLC-S**. The TOLC-S has 55 questions in 6 sections (Basic Mathematics; Reasoning, comprehension, problems; Biology; Chemistry; Physics; Earth Sciences) plus 30 English questions, in 120 + 15 minutes.

For the degree programme, the test is passed with **at least 5 points out of 20 in the Basic Mathematics section**. Anyone who scores below that gets the maths OFA. There are no other thresholds.

> [!NOTA] Physics
> If you scored less than 2/10 in the Physics section, the degree programme **recommends** (but does not require) that you take the physics catch-up course, also on www.ofa.unito.it. It is not an OFA and there is no exam to pass.

Sources: degree programme regulations (Regolamento didattico) for the 2026 cohort, article 2, paragraphs 7–11; the page [Admission requirements, TARM and TOLC](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=Requisiti_di_ammissione.html) (Requisiti di ammissione, TARM e TOLC).

## What you need to do

> [!METODO] From the transcript to the test
> 1. **Check your online transcript** on [MyUnito](https://my.unito.it): if “INT1475 OFA - MATEMATICA” is there, you have the OFA.
> 2. **Log in to [www.ofa.unito.it](https://www.ofa.unito.it)** with your UniTo SCU credentials (the same as for MyUnito). You must already be enrolled and have active student status.
> 3. **Enrol in the “OFA Matematica” course**: from the menu Corsi → Attività OFA (Courses → OFA activities), or [directly here](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20). Enrolment does not ask for a key.
> 4. **Study all the material** of the 8 modules: the exam questions are based on everything there is on the platform. The notes on this site follow the same modules; how the course is organised, and which typos were found in its material, is explained on the page [The official course](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/course.html).
> 5. **Register for an exam sitting** on MyUnito, Esami (exams) section, as soon as registration opens: only one per exam period, limited places.
> 6. **Arrive on time** at the Turing Lab with an identity document.

The degree programme recommends preparing straight away and aiming for the **autumn sittings**, so that you can move on faster with your studies: a solid grounding in the basics matters for everything you will study.

## The exam

From the degree programme's official instructions (January 2026 update):

| What | How it works |
|---|---|
| Questions | 5, worth 2 points each. Some are split into two sub-questions worth 1 point each; others, more complex, are worth 2 points with no sub-questions. |
| Formats | one choice out of four, multiple choice, true/false, numeric answer |
| Pass mark | 6 points out of 10 |
| Penalties | none for wrong answers: it pays to answer everything |
| Time | 45 minutes (1 hour with the extra time for SpLD [specific learning disorders, DSA] or disability) |
| Calculator | not allowed, whether scientific or basic (unless granted on a request made in time for SpLD or disability) |
| Scratch paper | one sheet handed out by the invigilators, collected at the end |
| Where | Turing Lab, on a computer, on the exam platform |
| Login | UniTo SCU credentials; **you cannot log in with SPID** (the Italian public digital identity) |
| What to bring | an identity document |
| Syllabus | all the material of the “OFA Matematica” course on the platform |

> [!TEST] What this means for how you study
> With 45 minutes for 5 questions you have about 9 minutes per question, without a calculator. The questions are real exercises (an inequality, a system, a logarithm, a trigonometric equation…), not definitions learnt by heart. Practise doing the calculations by hand and against the clock: the [mock tests](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/mock-tests.html) have the same format. The questions are in Italian, like the whole course and its material: the page [Italian for the OFA test](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/glossary.html) lists the Italian words and phrasings you will meet.

## Dates and registration

**2026/27.** As of 30 September 2026 the calendar has not been published yet. The degree programme publishes it on the page [Admission requirements](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=Requisiti_di_ammissione.html), in the section “Recupero obblighi formativi aggiuntivi (OFA) a.a. 2026-2027” (making up the additional learning requirements, academic year 2026-2027). The sittings also appear in the [exam listings on Esse3 (bacheca appelli)](https://esse3.unito.it/ListaAppelliOfferta.do), which you can consult without logging in.

> [!METODO] Finding the sittings on Esse3
> 1. Open the [exam listings](https://esse3.unito.it/ListaAppelliOfferta.do) and widen the date range (for example from today to the end of September of the following year).
> 2. Dipartimento (department): **[010088] INFORMATICA**.
> 3. Corso di studio (degree programme): **[0801L31] INFORMATICA**.
> 4. Attività didattica (course unit): **[INT1475] OFA - MATEMATICA**, then “Avvia ricerca” (start search).

**How it went in 2025/26.** Ten sittings, all in the Turing Lab (data from the Esse3 exam listings):

| Sitting | Registration | Registered / places |
|---|---|---|
| Monday 24 November 2025, 10:00 | 7–19 November | 45 / 60 |
| Monday 24 November 2025, 12:00 | 7–20 November | 19 / 60 |
| Friday 28 November 2025, 12:00 | 7–23 November | 46 / 60 |
| Wednesday 14 January 2026, 14:00 | 28 November – 9 January | 49 / 65 |
| Monday 19 January 2026, 15:00 | 28 November – 14 January | 52 / 60 |
| Monday 26 January 2026, 16:00 | 27 December – 21 January | 39 / 60 |
| Friday 29 May 2026, 14:00 | 27 February – 24 May | 57 / 60 |
| Thursday 4 June 2026, 11:00 | 27 February – 30 May | 50 / 60 |
| Thursday 3 September 2026, 14:00 | 1 July – 29 August | 23, no limit |
| Friday 18 September 2026, 14:00 | 20 July – 13 September | 23, no limit |

> [!TRAPPOLA] Only one sitting per exam period
> The instructions ask you to register for **one and only one** sitting per exam period, and the sittings have a limited number of places. In 2025/26 registration for the November sittings opened on 7 November, 17–21 days before the sittings: keep an eye on the dates and register straight away.

## Where

The test takes place in the **Turing Lab** of the **Department of Computer Science (Dipartimento di Informatica)**, **via Pessinetto 12, Turin**, on the raised ground floor (the same building as lecture rooms A and B). On Esse3 the room appears as “Lab Turing Informatica”. Map: [OpenStreetMap](https://www.openstreetmap.org/search?query=Via%20Pessinetto%2012%2C%20Torino).

## SpLD and disability

If you have a disability or an SpLD, when you register for the exam date you follow the same procedure as for other exams: the University's guidelines are on the page [Support for students with SpLD](https://www.unito.it/servizi/lo-studio/studenti-e-studentesse-con-disturbi-specifici-di-apprendimento-dsa/supporto) (Supporto per studenti con DSA) and the documentation must be sent to the lecturers of the sitting (list on the [degree programme's lecturers page](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/docenti.pl/Search?format=8&rs=1;title=Ricevimento%20studenti&max=5000)). The extra time is one third: **1 hour instead of 45 minutes**. A calculator can be allowed only for those who requested it within the deadlines.

## If you don't pass

- You try again in the **next exam period** (one sitting per exam period).
- The OFA must be passed **within the first year of your programme**. If you don't manage it, **you cannot have second-year exams recorded** until you pass it.
- You can take **first-year exams** even while the OFA is still outstanding.

> [!PROP] The rule for second-year exams
> The 2026/27 Degree Programme Guide (Guida del corso di laurea) says that exams of a year after the first can be recorded only after recording **at least 21 CFU (ECTS credits) of first-year exams** and **having passed the maths OFA** (for those who did not reach the TOLC-S threshold).

The degree programme regulations (article 9) set the same 21 CFU threshold; the OFA is added to it for those who have one.

## Help and contacts

| For | Contact |
|---|---|
| Questions about the OFA (rules, special cases) | OFA committee (commissione OFA), [commofa@educ.di.unito.it](mailto:commofa@educ.di.unito.it) |
| Problems logging in to the OFA platform | [platform helpdesk](https://www.ofa.unito.it/helpdesk/index.php?t=tcre) (“Crea ticket”, create ticket) |
| Practical advice from final-year students | first-year tutoring (tutorato matricole), [tutorato.informatica@unito.it](mailto:tutorato.informatica@unito.it), and the [1st-year Telegram group](https://t.me/+0sR7CugDQlllNzU0) indicated by the degree programme |
| Difficulties in your studies | [SUPERA](https://www.unito.it/servizi/pari-opportunita-benessere-e-assistenza/sportello-unito-la-riuscita-accademica-supera), the University's student support desk |

The degree programme also has an **individual tutoring** committee for first-year students, with a page on [informatica.i-learn.unito.it](https://informatica.i-learn.unito.it/) in which first-year students are enrolled automatically: look for the one for your academic year. All the information is on the [Tutoring](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=tutorato.html) (Tutorato) page.

## Frequently asked questions

**I scored 5/20 or more in Basic Mathematics: do I need to do anything?** No, you don't have the OFA.

**Can I make up the OFA by passing a first-year maths exam?** The official instructions give only one way: the course on the platform and its exam. If your case is special (for example you have transferred or come from another degree), ask the OFA committee.

**Is the course on the platform compulsory?** The degree programme “requires attendance and passing” of the catch-up course, i.e. studying its material and passing its exam. In any case the questions are based on that material: study all of it.

**Can I register for two sittings in the same exam period?** No: one and only one.

**Is the test on paper?** No, on a computer on the exam platform, with your SCU credentials. You only get one sheet of scratch paper.

**What happens with questions split into two parts?** Each part is worth 1 point: you can get one even if you get the other wrong.

## Sources

- Computer Science degree programme (corso di laurea in Informatica), [Admission requirements, TARM and TOLC](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=Requisiti_di_ammissione.html), academic year 2026/27: 5/20 threshold, making up the OFA with the course on www.ofa.unito.it and the in-person exam, committee contact.
- [Instructions for clearing the OFA, Computer Science degree programme](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1hiHaiepYJW2I5e-kx4gEYXZy3HCJBoNn/edit) (Istruzioni per assolvere l'OFA), January 2026 update, linked from the requirements page: exam format, registration for sittings, SpLD.
- [Degree programme regulations](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=regolamenti.html) (Regolamento didattico del corso di laurea), 2026 cohort, articles 2 and 9.
- [Degree Programme Guide 2026/27](https://laurea.informatica.unito.it/do/home.pl/View?doc=Guida_al_corso_di_laurea.html), pages 10–11: the 21 CFU and OFA rule for second-year exams.
- [Esse3 exam listings](https://esse3.unito.it/ListaAppelliOfferta.do), activity INT1475: 2025/26 sittings, room and registrations.
- OFA platform [www.ofa.unito.it](https://www.ofa.unito.it), “OFA Matematica” course: 8 modules, with books, exercises and online tests.

breve: "How the course on the OFA platform, which the test is built on, is organised: modules, units and activities, the notation it uses and the typos found in its material. Checked on 30 September 2026."
italian_original: https://github.com/DonFlammer/unito-ofa-matematica/blob/main/ai/corso-ufficiale.md
---

## In brief

- The catch-up course (corso di riallineamento) is called **“OFA Matematica”** (OFA: additional learning requirement, *obbligo formativo aggiuntivo*) and is on [www.ofa.unito.it](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20) (course number 20). You log in with your UniTo SCU credentials and enrol yourself, with no enrolment key.
- It has **8 modules**, divided into numbered **units** (1.1, 1.2, 2.1, …). For each unit there is a **Libro** (book), almost always an interactive **“Esplora”** (explore) worksheet, often a page of **Applicazioni** (applications), **Esercizi** and **Soluzioni** (exercises and solutions) as PDFs and an online **Test**; at the end of each module there is an **end-of-module test**.
- The test questions are based on **all the course material**: the notes in this guide follow the same modules and the same units, but they do not replace it.
- The material contains a few **typos**: you will find them at the bottom of this page, already verified. The notes have the correct version.

## The activities of each unit

| Activity | What it is | How to use it |
|---|---|---|
| Libro | the theory of the unit, divided into chapters, with worked examples | read it after the notes on the same topic, to get used to the course notation |
| Esplora | an interactive Maple worksheet, with graphs and calculations on the topic of the unit | use it to see what happens when a coefficient changes |
| Applicazioni | a page or a PDF with a real-world problem solved with the maths of the unit | read it as an extra worked example |
| Esercizi and Soluzioni | two PDFs: first the exercises, then the solutions (often just the result) | do them all by hand and check at the end |
| Test | an online test (Maple T.A.) on the unit; at the end of the module there is the end-of-module test | redo it until you get through it with no mistakes |

The book shows the formulas as images: if a formula is hard to read, zoom in on the page.

## The modules and the units

### Module 1 · Language, sets, logic and numbers

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=1) · notes: [module 1](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/01-language-sets-numbers.html)

- **1.1 Elementi di teoria degli insiemi e logica** (elements of set theory and logic): Libro; Esplora “Gli insiemi” (sets); Applicazioni “Un rompicapo tra logica e vita quotidiana” (a puzzle between logic and everyday life; PDF); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Ulteriori esercizi di logica” (further logic exercises); Test 1.1.
- **1.2 Numeri** (numbers): Libro; Esplora “Operazioni tra numeri” (operations with numbers); Applicazioni “Dal linguaggio quotidiano alla simbologia matematica” (from everyday language to mathematical symbols); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Ulteriori esercizi sui numeri” (further exercises on numbers); Test 1.2.
- Test modulo 1 (module 1 test).

### Module 2 · Polynomials and factorisation

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=2) · notes: [module 2](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/02-polynomials.html)

- **2.1 Polinomi e fattorizzazione** (polynomials and factorisation): Libro; Esplora “Polinomi e loro fattorizzazione” (polynomials and their factorisation); Applicazioni “La fattorizzazione di polinomi in matematica e non solo” (factorising polynomials, in mathematics and beyond); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Ulteriori esercizi sui polinomi” (further exercises on polynomials); Test 2.1.
- Test modulo 2.

### Module 3 · First- and second-degree equations and inequalities, systems

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=3) · notes: [module 3](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/03-equations-inequalities.html)

- **3.1 Equazioni e disequazioni algebriche di 1° grado con una incognita** (first-degree algebraic equations and inequalities in one unknown): Libro; Esplora “Le equazioni di 1° grado con una incognita” (first-degree equations in one unknown); Applicazioni “Un'equazione di 1° grado per esprimere l'età di tre fratelli” (a first-degree equation to express the ages of three siblings); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 3.1.
- **3.2 Equazioni e disequazioni algebriche di 2° grado con una incognita** (second-degree algebraic equations and inequalities in one unknown): Libro; Esplora “Le equazioni di 2° grado con una incognita” (second-degree equations in one unknown); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Applicazione di equazioni di 2° grado alla risoluzione di problemi” (applying second-degree equations to problem solving); Test 3.2.
- **3.3 Sistemi di equazioni** (systems of equations): Libro; Applicazione “Alla ricerca di un numero che soddisfi le richieste” (looking for a number that meets the requirements); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Sistemi lineari di equazioni” (linear systems of equations).
- Test modulo 3.

### Module 4 · Rational, radical and absolute value equations and inequalities

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=4) · notes: [module 4](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/04-rational-radical-absolute-value.html)

- **4.1 Equazioni e disequazioni algebriche fratte** (rational algebraic equations and inequalities): Libro; Esplora; Applicazione “Le equazioni fratte e la realtà: un binomio possibile?” (rational equations and reality: a possible pairing?); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 4.1.
- **4.2 Equazioni e disequazioni irrazionali** (radical equations and inequalities): Libro; Esplora “Le disequazioni irrazionali” (radical inequalities); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 4.2.
- **4.3 Equazioni e disequazioni con valore assoluto** (absolute value equations and inequalities): Libro; Esplora; Applicazione “Una disequazione con valore assoluto per esprimere la temperatura corporea” (an absolute value inequality to express body temperature); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 4.3.
- Test finale modulo 4 (module 4 final test).

### Module 5 · Analytic geometry: lines and conics

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=5) · notes: [module 5](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/05-analytic-geometry.html)

- **Introduzione: il piano cartesiano** (introduction: the Cartesian plane; one page).
- **5.1 La retta nel piano cartesiano** (the straight line in the Cartesian plane): Libro; Esplora; Applicazioni “Quale parcheggio è più conveniente?” (which car park is cheaper?); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Ulteriori esercizi su piano cartesiano e retta” (further exercises on the Cartesian plane and the straight line); Test 5.1.
- **5.2 Le coniche** (conics): Libro (circle, parabola, ellipse, hyperbola); Esplora “La parabola nel piano cartesiano” (the parabola in the Cartesian plane); Applicazioni “La parabola per la risoluzione di disequazioni” (using the parabola to solve inequalities); Curiosità (fun fact) “Perché le coniche si chiamano ‘coniche’?” (why are conics called ‘conics’?); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 5.2.
- Test fine modulo 5 (end-of-module test, module 5).

### Module 6 · Real functions of a real variable

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=6) · notes: [module 6](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/06-functions.html)

- **Nota introduttiva** (introductory note; PDF).
- **6.1 Definizione di funzione e principali caratteristiche** (definition of a function and main properties): Libro; Esplora “Grafici di funzione” (graphs of functions); Applicazioni “Legame tra grafici ed equazioni” (the link between graphs and equations); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 6.1.
- **6.2 Esempi di funzioni utili** (examples of useful functions): Libro; Esplora “Il valore assoluto di una funzione” (the absolute value of a function); Applicazioni “Proporzionalità diretta e inversa in fisica” (direct and inverse proportionality in physics); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 6.2.
- Test fine modulo 6.

### Module 7 · Exponentials and logarithms

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=7) · notes: [module 7](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/07-exponentials-logarithms.html)

- **7.1 Esponenziali e logaritmi** (exponentials and logarithms): Libro; Nota (note) “La base $e$, la base $10$” (the base $e$, the base $10$); Esplora “La funzione esponenziale” (the exponential function) and “La funzione logaritmica” (the logarithmic function); Applicazioni “Esponenziali per modellizzare la realtà” (exponentials to model reality); Esercizi and Soluzioni “Ulteriori esercizi sui logaritmi” (further exercises on logarithms); Test 7.1.
- **7.2 Equazioni e disequazioni esponenziali e logaritmiche** (exponential and logarithmic equations and inequalities): Libro; Applicazioni “Il pH di una soluzione chimica” (the pH of a chemical solution); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 7.2.
- Test fine modulo 7.

### Module 8 · Trigonometry

[Module section on the platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20&section=8) · notes: [module 8](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/modules/08-trigonometry.html)

- **Introduzione: circonferenza goniometrica e angoli** (introduction: the unit circle and angles; one page).
- **8.1 Funzioni trigonometriche** (trigonometric functions): Libro; Esplora “La funzione trigonometrica seno” (the sine function) and “La funzione trigonometrica coseno” (the cosine function); Applicazioni “Legami con la fisica” (links with physics); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 8.1.
- **8.2 Equazioni e disequazioni trigonometriche** (trigonometric equations and inequalities): Libro; Applicazioni “Trigonometria e triangoli” (trigonometry and triangles); Esercizi and Soluzioni; Test 8.2.
- Test fine modulo 8.

## The course notation

The notes use the same notation, so there are no surprises in the test:

- trigonometric functions $\sin x$, $\cos x$, $\tan x$ and $\operatorname{cotan} x$; natural logarithm $\ln x$, logarithm to base $a$ $\log_a x$;
- intervals with round brackets (endpoint excluded) and square brackets (endpoint included): $(-2, 3]$, $(-\infty, 1)$;
- in solutions: $\forall x \in \R$ (every real number), $\nexists x \in \R$ (no solution), $\vee$ for “or” (oppure); “t.c.” for “such that” (tale che);
- in trigonometry exercises the general solutions are written with $k \in \Z$, for example $x = \frac{\pi}{4} + k\pi$.

## Typos in the material

Recalculating the course exercises to write the notes turned up a few typos. Each one was double-checked with symbolic computation and **compared with the original page or PDF** on 30 September 2026. The notes have the correct version. The list only includes the verified typos and is not a complete review of the material; in module 3 none were found. If you find others you can report them to the [platform helpdesk](https://www.ofa.unito.it/helpdesk/index.php?t=tcre).

### Module 1

- **Libro 1.1**, proof by contradiction that if $n^2$ is even then $n$ is even: with $n = 2p + 1$ we get $n^2 = 4p^2 + 4p + 1$, not $4p^2 + 2p + 1$. The conclusion ($n^2$ is odd) is still correct.
- **Libro 1.1**, example on how to state a theorem: two real numbers with the same absolute value are equal **or** opposite ($a = b$ or $a = -b$), not “equal and opposite” (uguali ed opposti).
- **Libro 1.2**, extension from $\Z$ to $\Q$: the equation $ax = b$ has the solution $x = \frac{b}{a}$ when $a \ne 0$; the book writes $b \ne 0$.
- **Soluzioni 1.1, exercise 2**, items iv and v: the negation of “all children are more than 6 years old” is “at least one child is **at most** 6 years old”, not “less than 6 years old” (a child of exactly 6 makes the original sentence false). In the same way, the negation of “there is a building with more than 10 floors” is “every building has at most 10 floors”.
- **Soluzioni 1.2, exercise 1**: the largest four-digit number in which units plus tens equals hundreds plus thousands is $9999$ ($9 + 9 = 9 + 9$), not $9054$. Even requiring all the digits to be different, there are numbers larger than $9054$, for example $9687$.
- **Soluzioni 1.2, exercise 5**: $\frac{27}{8}$ is not equal to $\frac{9}{2}$; an equivalent fraction is $\frac{27}{6}$.

### Module 2

- **Libro 2.1**, taking out the common factor: $15x^3 + 3x^2 - 6x = 3x(5x^2 + x - 2)$, not $3x(5x^3 + x^2 - 2)$.
- **Soluzioni 2.1, exercise 3**: $(2x^3 - 10x^2 + 3x - 15) : (x - 5)$ gives the quotient $2x^2 + 3$ (remainder $0$), not $2x^3 + 3$.
- **Soluzioni 2.1, exercise 6**: $8x^3 + 36x^2 + 54x + 27 = (2x + 3)^3$, not $(2x + 3)^2$: the text itself talks about the cube of a binomial.

### Module 4

- **Libro 4.3, example 4** ($|x + 1| + |x - 2| \le 5$): the first system gives $-2 \le x < -1$, not $-2 \le x < 1$. The final result, $-2 \le x \le 3$, is correct.
- **Soluzioni 4.2, exercise 6** ($\sqrt{7x + 2} + \sqrt{16 - x^2} > -5$): the solution is the domain, $-\frac{2}{7} \le x \le 4$, not “for every real $x$”. The reasoning on the sheet (a sum of square roots is never negative) is correct, but it only holds where the roots exist.

### Module 5

- **Libro 5.2**, circle, tangent line to $x^2 + y^2 + 6x - 4y = 0$ at the point $P_0 = (-6, 4)$: the line through $P_0$ with slope $\frac{3}{2}$ is written $y - 4 = \frac{3}{2}(x + 6)$. The book writes $y - 2 = \frac{3}{2}(x + 3)$, which passes through the centre $(-3, 2)$; the final result, $y = \frac{3}{2}x + 13$, is correct.

### Module 6

- **Libro 6.1**, translations: $\cos\left(\frac{x}{2} + 1\right)$ is obtained from $\cos\frac{x}{2}$ by a translation of **2** units to the left, not one, because $\cos\left(\frac{x}{2} + 1\right) = \cos\frac{x + 2}{2}$. In the same way, $\cos\left(\frac{x}{2} - 1\right)$ is shifted 2 units to the right. The vertical shifts ($\pm 1$) are correct.
- **Soluzioni 6.1, exercise 1**: the domain of $f(x) = \sqrt{2x^2 - x - 3}$ is $x \le -1$ or $x \ge \frac{3}{2}$, not $x \le 1$ or $x \ge \frac{3}{2}$.

### Module 7

- **Soluzioni 7.2, exercise 6** ($\left(\frac{1}{3}\right)^{x^2 - 1} < 9^{2x}$): the solution is $x < -2 - \sqrt{5}$ or $x > -2 + \sqrt{5}$. The sheet writes $x < -1 - \sqrt{5}$ in the first part; the second is correct.

### Module 8

- **Libro 8.2**, inequalities, example 2 ($\cos 2x < 1$ in $[0, 2\pi]$): $\cos 2x = 1$ when $2x$ equals $0$, $2\pi$ or $4\pi$, i.e. for $x = 0$, $x = \pi$ and $x = 2\pi$. The solution is $0 < x < \pi$ or $\pi < x < 2\pi$; the book only excludes $0$ and $2\pi$.
- **Soluzioni 8.2, exercise 10** ($\tan x \ge 1$ in $[0, 2\pi]$): the solution is $\frac{\pi}{4} \le x < \frac{\pi}{2}$ or $\frac{5}{4}\pi \le x < \frac{3}{2}\pi$. The one on the sheet ($0 \le x < \frac{\pi}{2}$, $\frac{3}{4}\pi \le x < \frac{3}{2}\pi$, $\frac{7}{4}\pi \le x \le 2\pi$) is the solution of $\tan x \ge -1$.

> File: `ai/italian-glossary.md`

---
titolo: "Italian for the OFA test"
breve: "The Italian words and phrasings you will meet in the “OFA Matematica” course and in the test, module by module, with their meaning in English."
---

The “OFA Matematica” course, the OFA platform (www.ofa.unito.it) and the test are all in Italian. This page lists the Italian words and phrases you will meet: first the key terms of each of the 8 modules, then how the test questions are phrased, and finally how numbers and symbols are written. Nouns are given with their Italian article (il, lo, la, l', i, gli, le) where it helps.

## Module 1 · Language, sets, logic and numbers

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| l'insieme, l'elemento | set, element |
| appartiene a ($\in$); non appartiene a ($\notin$) | belongs to; does not belong to |
| l'insieme vuoto ($\varnothing$); l'insieme universo | empty set; universal set |
| per elenco; con una proprietà caratteristica | roster (listing) notation; set-builder notation |
| il diagramma di Eulero-Venn | Venn diagram |
| il sottoinsieme (proprio / improprio); è contenuto in ($\subseteq$) | (proper / improper) subset; is contained in |
| l'insieme delle parti ($\mathcal{P}(A)$) | power set |
| l'unione ($\cup$), l'intersezione ($\cap$), la differenza ($\setminus$), il complementare | union, intersection, difference, complement |
| la differenza simmetrica ($\Delta$) | symmetric difference |
| il prodotto cartesiano ($A \times B$); la coppia ordinata | Cartesian product; ordered pair |
| la proposizione; il predicato | statement (proposition); predicate |
| vero, falso; il valore di verità; la tavola di verità | true, false; truth value; truth table |
| i connettivi: la congiunzione (e, $\wedge$), la disgiunzione (o, $\vee$), la negazione (non, $\neg$) | connectives: conjunction (and), disjunction (or), negation (not) |
| l'implicazione (se… allora…, $\Rightarrow$); la doppia implicazione (se e solo se, $\Leftrightarrow$) | implication (if… then…); biconditional (if and only if) |
| il quantificatore universale (per ogni, $\forall$) / esistenziale (esiste, $\exists$) | universal (for every) / existential (there exists) quantifier |
| la condizione necessaria / sufficiente | necessary / sufficient condition |
| l'ipotesi, la tesi | hypothesis, conclusion (thesis) |
| la contronominale | contrapositive |
| la dimostrazione per assurdo; il controesempio | proof by contradiction; counterexample |
| almeno uno; al più; nessuno | at least one; at most; none |
| i numeri naturali ($\N$), interi ($\Z$), razionali ($\Q$), irrazionali, reali ($\R$) | natural numbers, integers, rational, irrational and real numbers |
| pari, dispari; il numero primo | even, odd; prime number |
| il divisore, il multiplo; il MCD, il mcm | divisor, multiple; gcd (greatest common divisor), lcm (least common multiple) |
| la frazione ridotta ai minimi termini | fraction in lowest terms |
| il numero decimale finito / periodico; il periodo, l'antiperiodo | terminating / repeating decimal; period (the repeating block), the non-repeating digits between the decimal point and the period |
| la frazione generatrice | fraction form (of a repeating decimal) |
| la percentuale, lo sconto, l'aumento | percentage, discount, increase |
| la potenza, la base, l'esponente; la radice quadrata, il radicale, il radicando; razionalizzare | power, base, exponent; square root, radical, radicand; rationalise (the denominator) |
| l'intervallo aperto / chiuso; l'estremo incluso / escluso; la semiretta | open / closed interval; endpoint included / excluded; ray (half-line) |

## Module 2 · Polynomials and factorisation

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| il monomio; il coefficiente; la parte letterale | monomial; coefficient; literal part (the letters with their exponents) |
| i monomi simili | like terms |
| il polinomio; il grado | polynomial; degree |
| il polinomio ordinato / completo | polynomial written in order of powers / complete polynomial (no power missing) |
| il termine noto | constant term |
| il coefficiente direttivo | leading coefficient |
| i prodotti notevoli | special products |
| il quadrato di binomio; il doppio prodotto | square of a binomial; the term $2ab$ (twice the product) |
| il cubo di binomio | cube of a binomial |
| la differenza di quadrati | difference of two squares |
| la somma / differenza di cubi | sum / difference of cubes |
| il falso quadrato ($a^2 \pm ab + b^2$) | “false square”: the quadratic factor of a sum or difference of cubes |
| la divisione tra polinomi | polynomial division |
| il dividendo, il divisore, il quoziente, il resto | dividend, divisor, quotient, remainder |
| la regola di Ruffini | Ruffini's rule (synthetic division) |
| il teorema del resto | remainder theorem |
| il teorema di Ruffini | factor theorem (Ruffini's theorem) |
| la radice (lo zero) di un polinomio | root (zero) of a polynomial |
| divisibile per | divisible by |
| scomporre in fattori; la scomposizione (la fattorizzazione) | factorise; factorisation |
| il raccoglimento totale (a fattor comune) | taking out the common factor |
| il raccoglimento parziale | factorising by grouping |
| il trinomio somma e prodotto (il trinomio notevole) | quadratic trinomial (sum and product method) |
| il polinomio irriducibile | irreducible polynomial |
| la frazione algebrica | algebraic fraction |
| le condizioni di esistenza (C.E.) | domain conditions |
| semplificare | simplify (cancel common factors) |
| la legge di annullamento del prodotto | zero-product property |

## Module 3 · First- and second-degree equations and inequalities, systems

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| l'equazione; l'incognita | equation; unknown |
| il primo membro, il secondo membro | left-hand side, right-hand side |
| la soluzione (la radice) | solution (root) |
| i principi di equivalenza | equivalence principles (the rules for transforming an equation) |
| l'equazione determinata / indeterminata / impossibile | determined (one solution) / indeterminate (infinitely many) / impossible (no solution) equation |
| l'identità | identity (true for every value) |
| l'equazione di primo grado / di secondo grado | first-degree (linear) / second-degree (quadratic) equation |
| la forma normale ($ax^2 + bx + c = 0$) | standard form |
| l'equazione pura ($b = 0$) / spuria ($c = 0$) / completa | incomplete quadratic with $b = 0$ / incomplete quadratic with $c = 0$ / complete quadratic |
| la formula risolutiva; la formula ridotta | quadratic formula; reduced formula (for even $b$) |
| il discriminante ($\Delta$) | discriminant |
| due soluzioni reali distinte / coincidenti | two distinct / coincident (equal) real solutions |
| nessuna soluzione reale | no real solution |
| la somma e il prodotto delle radici | sum and product of the roots |
| il completamento del quadrato | completing the square |
| la disequazione | inequality |
| il verso della disequazione; cambiare il verso | direction of the inequality; reverse the direction |
| l'equazione associata | associated equation (the same expression with $=$ instead of the inequality sign) |
| la parabola rivolta verso l'alto / verso il basso | parabola opening upwards / downwards |
| gli intervalli esterni / interni | the intervals outside / between the roots |
| il sistema (lineare) | (linear) system |
| il metodo di sostituzione / del confronto / di riduzione | substitution / comparison / elimination method |
| la regola di Cramer | Cramer's rule |
| il sistema determinato / indeterminato / impossibile | determined / indeterminate / impossible system |
| il sistema di disequazioni; la doppia disequazione | system of inequalities; double inequality |
| il parametro | parameter |
| il problema a parole | word problem |

## Module 4 · Rational, radical and absolute value equations and inequalities

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| l'equazione fratta, la disequazione fratta | rational (fractional) equation, rational inequality |
| il numeratore, il denominatore | numerator, denominator |
| le condizioni di esistenza (C.E.) | domain conditions |
| il campo di esistenza | domain |
| il denominatore si annulla | the denominator is zero |
| la forma intera | the equation without denominators (after multiplying by the lcm) |
| portare tutto al primo membro | bring everything to the left-hand side |
| lo studio del segno; la tabella dei segni | sign study; sign chart (sign table) |
| la regola dei segni | rule of signs |
| positivo, negativo, nullo | positive, negative, zero |
| l'equazione irrazionale, la disequazione irrazionale | radical (irrational) equation, radical inequality |
| il radicale, il radicando | radical, radicand |
| l'indice pari / dispari | even / odd index (of a root) |
| elevare al quadrato (entrambi i membri) | square (both sides) |
| la soluzione estranea | extraneous solution |
| la soluzione accettabile / da scartare (non accettabile) | acceptable solution / solution to discard |
| la verifica | check (substituting the solution back) |
| il valore assoluto, $\lvert x \rvert$ | absolute value |
| il modulo | absolute value (the same word also means “module”) |
| la distanza da zero; la distanza tra $x$ e $a$ ($\lvert x - a \rvert$) | distance from zero; distance between $x$ and $a$ |
| distinguere i casi; per casi | split into cases; case by case |
| maggiore / minore (o uguale) | greater / less than (or equal to) |
| non negativo ($\ge 0$) | non-negative |
| per ogni $x$ del campo di esistenza | for every $x$ in the domain |

## Module 5 · Analytic geometry: lines and conics

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| il piano cartesiano; il riferimento cartesiano | Cartesian plane; Cartesian coordinate system |
| l'origine; gli assi | origin; axes |
| l'asse delle ascisse / delle ordinate | x-axis / y-axis |
| l'ascissa, l'ordinata | x-coordinate (abscissa), y-coordinate (ordinate) |
| i quadranti | quadrants |
| la distanza tra due punti; il punto medio | distance between two points; midpoint |
| la retta | (straight) line |
| la forma esplicita ($y = mx + q$) / implicita ($ax + by + c = 0$) | slope-intercept form / general (implicit) form |
| il coefficiente angolare (la pendenza) | slope (gradient) |
| l'ordinata all'origine | y-intercept |
| la retta per un punto / per due punti | line through a point / through two points |
| le rette parallele / perpendicolari / incidenti / coincidenti | parallel / perpendicular / intersecting / coincident lines |
| l'antireciproco ($-\frac{1}{m}$) | negative reciprocal |
| il fascio di rette (proprio / improprio) | pencil of lines (through a point / all parallel) |

| l'asse di un segmento; la bisettrice | perpendicular bisector of a segment; bisector (of an angle, of the quadrants) |

## Module 7 · Exponentials and logarithms

## How test questions are phrased

These are the phrasings used by the entry test and the mock tests of the Italian original of this guide; they are typical of Italian maths questions.

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| Qual è…? | What is…? |
| Quali delle seguenti affermazioni sono vere? | Which of the following statements are true? |
| Quali dei seguenti numeri sono radici del polinomio…? | Which of the following numbers are roots of the polynomial…? |
| Vero o falso: … | True or false: … |
| Quanto vale…? | What is the value of…? (What is…?) |
| Quanto misura…? · Quanto misura, in cm, il lato più lungo? | How long is… (what is the size of…)? · How long, in cm, is the longer side? |
| Quanti…? · Quante soluzioni ha nell'intervallo $[0, 2\pi)$? | How many…? · How many solutions does it have in the interval $[0, 2\pi)$? |
| Quanti numeri interi soddisfano la disequazione…? | How many integers satisfy the inequality…? |
| Qual è il resto della divisione di… per…? | What is the remainder when… is divided by…? |
| Lo sviluppo di… è | The expansion of… is |
| Le soluzioni della disequazione… sono | The solutions of the inequality… are |
| L'insieme delle soluzioni della disequazione… è | The solution set of the inequality… is |
| L'equazione… ha come soluzioni | The solutions of the equation… are |
| Ha una sola soluzione reale. Quanto vale? | It has only one real solution. What is it? |
| La sua negazione è | Its negation is |
| Il dominio della funzione… è | The domain of the function… is |
| La retta che passa per $P$ ed è perpendicolare alla retta… ha equazione | The line that passes through $P$ and is perpendicular to the line… has the equation |
| Per quale valore di $k$…? · Per quali valori di $k$…? | For what value of $k$…? · For what values of $k$…? |
| …è tangente alla circonferenza… | …is tangent to the circle… |
| Considera l'equazione… · Considera la funzione… | Consider the equation… · Consider the function… |
| Sia $A$ l'insieme… · Siano $f(x) = …$ e $g(x) = …$ | Let $A$ be the set… · Let $f(x) = …$ and $g(x) = …$ |
| Chiama $x$ la lunghezza… · Indica con $\alpha$ l'angolo… | Call $x$ the length… · Let $\alpha$ denote the angle… |
| Nel piano cartesiano sono dati i punti… | In the Cartesian plane the points… are given |
| …appartengono sia alla parabola sia alla retta? | …belong to both the parabola and the line? |
| …è equidistante da $A$ e da $B$ | …is equidistant from $A$ and $B$ |
| …è divisibile per… | …is divisible by… |
| …se e solo se… | …if and only if… |
| due soluzioni reali coincidenti / distinte | two equal (coincident) / distinct real solutions |
| non ha soluzioni reali · nessuna soluzione reale | has no real solutions · no real solution |
| solo $x = 9$ | only $x = 9$ |
| Di che percentuale è diminuito il prezzo finale rispetto a quello iniziale? | By what percentage has the final price decreased compared with the initial one? |
| Scrivi solo il numero | Write only the number |
| Per $x \ne \pm 2$, l'espressione… è uguale a | For $x \ne \pm 2$, the expression… is equal to |

Other phrasings and instructions common in Italian exercises and online quizzes (not all of them necessarily appear in the test):

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| Quale delle seguenti…? | Which (one) of the following…? |
| Risolvi… | Solve… |
| Determina… · Trova… | Find… |
| Calcola… | Calculate… (work out…) |
| Semplifica… | Simplify… |
| Scomponi in fattori… | Factorise… |
| Verifica che… | Check (verify) that… |
| Dimostra che… | Prove that… |
| Stabilisci se… | Decide whether… |
| Scrivi la soluzione come intervallo | Write the solution as an interval |
| Disegna il grafico… · Rappresenta… | Draw the graph… · Represent… |
| Scegli un'alternativa · Scegli una o più alternative | Choose one option · Choose one or more options |
| Seleziona tutte le risposte corrette | Select all the correct answers |
| Nessuna delle precedenti | None of the above |
| Risposta · Punteggio · Tempo rimasto | Answer · Score · Time left |
| Termina il tentativo | Finish the attempt |

## Numbers and notation

| Italian | English |
|---|---|
| `0,75` · `3,5` · `9,50 €` | 0.75 · 3.5 · €9.50: Italian uses a comma for decimals |
| `1.000` · `10.000` (also `1 000`) | 1,000 · 10,000: a point (or a space) separates thousands |
| `a : b` | a ÷ b (the colon means division) |
| `a · b` | a × b |
| `P(1; 2)` | the point (1, 2): some books separate coordinates with a semicolon, because the comma is the decimal separator |
| oppure, o ($\vee$) | or: “$x < -1$ oppure $x > 2$” means $x < -1$ or $x > 2$ |
| e ($\wedge$) | and |
| $\forall$ per ogni | for every (for all) |
| $\exists$ esiste; $\nexists$ non esiste | there exists; there is no |
| t.c. (tale che), also written $\mid$ or `:` | such that |
| C.E. (condizioni di esistenza) | domain conditions |
| $(a, b)$ · $[a, b]$ · $[a, b)$ | intervals: round bracket = endpoint excluded, square bracket = endpoint included |
| $]a, b[$ | the same as $(a, b)$ in some books |
| $+\infty$, $-\infty$: più infinito, meno infinito | plus infinity, minus infinity |
| `sen x` · `tg x` · `cotg x`, `ctg x` | sin x · tan x · cot x: other Italian spellings you may meet; the course writes $\sin x$, $\tan x$, $\operatorname{cotan} x$ |
| $\ln x$ · $\log_a x$ · `log x` | natural logarithm · logarithm to base a · without a base, log can mean base 10 or base e depending on the book |
| $30°$: trenta gradi | 30 degrees |
| MCD, mcm | gcd, lcm |
| con $k$ intero ($k \in \Z$) | with $k$ an integer |

<!-- FILE: ai/study-plan.md -->
> File: `ai/study-plan.md`

---
titolo: "Your study plan"
breve: "A week-by-week plan up to the day of the test, with the method for studying each module and the strategy for the 45 minutes."
italian_original: https://github.com/DonFlammer/unito-ofa-matematica/blob/main/ai/piano-di-studio.md
---

## Where to start

1. Take the [entry test](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/entry-test.html): 24 questions, about 30 minutes, without a calculator. At the end you see your result module by module, and the plan above gives more time to the modules where you did worst.
2. Choose the exam date to aim for. The degree programme recommends the **autumn sittings** (in 2025/26 they were on 24 and 28 November): the sooner you catch up on the basics, the faster you move on with your studies.
3. Decide how many hours a week you can put in, on top of lectures. If you start with serious gaps you need about 75 hours in all: sixty or so for the eight modules, the rest for the mock tests and revision. That means about 10 hours a week for 8 weeks, or 6 hours a week for 13 weeks. If some modules go well in the entry test, the plan takes time away from them.
4. Follow the modules **in order**: each module uses the ones before it (rational equations use factorisation, logarithmic inequalities use second-degree ones, and so on).

## How to study a module

> [!METODO] A module in six steps
> 1. **Read the notes** for the module with pen and paper. When you reach a worked example, cover it and try to do it on your own, then compare.
> 2. **Open the Libro (book) for the same module** on the [OFA platform](https://www.ofa.unito.it/course/view.php?id=20) (OFA: additional learning requirement, *obbligo formativo aggiuntivo*): it is the material the test is built on. It also helps you get used to the notation you will find.
> 3. **Exercises**: first those in the notes, from basic level to test level, then the “Esercizi” (exercises) PDFs on the platform, checking with the “Soluzioni” (solutions) PDFs.
> 4. **Online tests on the platform**: the “Test” of each unit and the “Test modulo” (module test). Redo it until you get through it with no mistakes.
> 5. **Quizzes and checklist** at the end of the notes. If a checklist item is not clear to you, reread that section.
> 6. **Spaced revision**: every two modules, 20 minutes on the [formula sheet](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/formulas.html) for the previous modules.

> [!TRAPPOLA] Reading is not enough
> Rereading the notes gives you the impression that you have understood, but in the test you need to be able to do the calculations on your own, by hand and with the clock ticking. For every hour of reading, at least one hour of exercises.

## An example: eight weeks to the end of November

This is the plan the site suggests if you start at the end of September with 10 hours a week and aim for a sitting at the end of November. The plan at the top of the page recalculates it for your dates and your hours.

| Week | What to do |
|---|---|
| 1 | online transcript (libretto), enrolment in the OFA course, entry test; module 1 (language, sets, numbers) |
| 2 | module 2 (polynomials and factorisation); module 3, first- and second-degree equations and inequalities |
| 3 | module 3, systems; module 4, rational and radical equations and inequalities |
| 4 | module 4, absolute value; module 5, Cartesian plane, straight line and the first conics |
| 5 | end of module 5; module 6 (functions) |
| 6 | **register for the sitting** as soon as registration opens; module 7 (exponentials and logarithms); start of module 8 |
| 7 | module 8 (trigonometry); mock test 1 |
| 8 | mock tests 2–8 and targeted revision of the modules you got wrong; the day after, the test |

## Without a calculator

In the test the numbers are chosen so that the calculations can be done by hand, but you need to be quick. Train like this right from the start:

- **Fractions**: simplify before multiplying; to add, use the least common multiple, not the product of the denominators.
- **Powers and roots**: learn by heart the squares up to $20^2$, the cubes up to $10^3$, the powers of 2 up to $2^{10} = 1024$, the powers of 3 up to $3^5 = 243$.
- **Logarithms**: recognise the powers: $\log_2 32 = 5$ because $2^5 = 32$; $\log_{10} 0.001 = -3$.
- **Trigonometry**: you must know the values of sine, cosine and tangent for $0$, $\frac{\pi}{6}$, $\frac{\pi}{4}$, $\frac{\pi}{3}$, $\frac{\pi}{2}$ without thinking; the others are worked out from the unit circle.
- **Checking**: before choosing an answer, estimate the order of magnitude and substitute the result into the original equation. In multiple-choice quizzes, this is often the quickest way.

## During the 45 minutes

> [!METODO] Strategy for the test
> 1. **Read all 5 questions** (one minute): work out which ones you can do straight away.
> 2. **Start with the easiest**: scoring points early takes the anxiety away.
> 3. **Keep up the pace**: about 9 minutes per question. If after 12 minutes you are still stuck, move on to the next one and come back later.
> 4. **Two-part questions**: each part is worth 1 point. If the second is hard, at least make sure of the first.
> 5. **Numeric answers**: write the number in the form requested (integer, decimal or fraction) and double-check the sign.
> 6. **No penalties**: never leave an answer blank; if you don't know, rule out the impossible options and choose.
> 7. **Last 5 minutes**: double-check the domain conditions and the directions of the inequalities, where most points are lost.

## If you get stuck

- Reread the section of the notes and redo the worked examples before the exercises.
- Ask the first-year tutors ([tutorato.informatica@unito.it](mailto:tutorato.informatica@unito.it)) or in the first-year Telegram group: someone is often preparing for the same sitting.
- For doubts about the OFA rules: [commofa@educ.di.unito.it](mailto:commofa@educ.di.unito.it). All the contacts are on the page [Rules, dates and contacts](https://donflammer.github.io/unito-ofa-maths/rules.html#help-and-contacts).
